## User

what is doctest?

## Assistant

A doctest is a code example in Rust documentation comments (`///`) that gets compiled and run as a test.

```rust
/// Adds two numbers together.
///
/// ```
/// let result = my_crate::add(2, 3);
/// assert_eq!(result, 5);
/// ```
pub fn add(a: i32, b: i32) -> i32 {
    a + b
}
```

The code block inside the doc comment is automatically extracted and run by `cargo test`. The ignored one in your project is in [src/keymap/mod.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/keymap/mod.rs) - likely marked `ignore` because it's illustrative rather than executable.

  }\n16: \n17:         AppMsg::SaveFile => {\n18:             let file_path = model.document().file_path.clone();\n19:             match file_path {\n20:                 Some(path) => {\n21: ## Assistant

### Coverage Analysis Summary

#### Not Meaningful to Test (runtime/rendering code)
| File | Coverage | Reason |
|------|----------|--------|
| `runtime/app.rs` | 0% | Event loop, window management - requires GUI |
| `runtime/perf.rs` | 0% | Performance metrics - runtime only |
| `view/mod.rs` | 0% | Rendering orchestration |
| `view/frame.rs` | 35% | Pixel buffer operations |
| `debug_dump.rs` | 0% | Debug utilities |
| `debug_overlay.rs` | 0% | Debug UI overlay |

#### Potentially Meaningful Gaps

| File | Coverage | Assessment |
|------|----------|------------|
| `messages.rs` | 0% | **False positive** - just enum definitions, no logic |
| `commands.rs` | 6% | Mostly static data (`COMMANDS` registry), `filter_commands` is testable |
| `update/app.rs` | 14% | File I/O handlers - hard to test without mocking fs |
| `keymap/command.rs` | 16% | `to_msgs()` mappings - could add more coverage but largely mechanical |
| `update/ui.rs` | 34% | Modal handlers - **good candidate** for more tests |
| `config.rs` | 34% | Already has tests; remaining is file I/O |
| `model/mod.rs` | 39% | Accessor methods; higher-level tests exercise these |
| `overlay.rs` | 39% | Rendering helpers |

#### Recommendation

The codebase has **excellent coverage where it matters** (text editing, cursor movement, multi-cursor, selections, undo/redo). The low-coverage files are mostly:
1. **Runtime/rendering** - inherently hard to unit test
2. **Static data** - no logic to test
3. **I/O operations** - would need mocking infrastructure

**One actionable area**: `update/ui.rs` modal handlers (command palette, goto line, find/replace) could benefit from more tests for edge cases like input validation, filtering, and state transitions.

   assert!(model.ui.active_modal.is_some());\n    assert_eq!(\n        model.ui.active_modal.as_ref().unwrap().id(),\n        ModalId::CommandPalette\n    );\n}\n\n#[test]\nfn test_open_goto_line_message() {\n    let mut model = test_model(\"hello\\n\", 0, 0);\n\n    update(&mut model, Msg::Ui(UiMsg::Modal(ModalMsg::OpenGotoLine)));\n\n |\n|[.markdownlint-cli2.yaml](/taiki-e/cargo-llvm-cov/blob/main/.markdownlint-cli2.yaml \".markdownlint-cli2.yaml\")\n ... \nThis is a wrapper around rustc [`-C instrument-coverage`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/rustc/instrument-coverage.html) and provides:\n\n* Generate very precise coverage data. (line, region, and branch coverage. branch coverage is currently optional and requires nightly, see [\\](https://github.com/taiki-e/cargo-llvm-cov/issues/8) for more)\n* Support `cargo test` , `cargo run` , and [`cargo nextest`](https://nexte.st/book/test-coverage.html) with command-line interface compatible with cargo. * Support for proc-macro, including coverage of UI tests. * Support for doc tests. (this is currently optional and requires nightly, see [\\](https://github.com/taiki-e/cargo-llvm-cov/issues/2) for more)\n* Fast because it does not introduce extra layers between rustc, cargo, and llvm-tools. **Table of Contents:**\n\n## Usage\n\n[]()\n\n### Basic usage\n\n[]()\n\nBy default, run tests (via `cargo test` ), and print the coverage summary to stdout.\n ... \n```\ncargo llvm-cov clean --workspace #  remove artifacts that may affect the coverage results \ncargo llvm-cov --no-report --features a\ncargo llvm-cov --no-report --features b\ncargo llvm-cov report --lcov #  generate report without tests\n```\n\nTip\n\nTo include coverage for doctests you also need to pass `--doctests` to `cargo llvm-cov report` . ### Get coverage of C/C++ code linked to Rust library/binary\n\n[]()\n\nSet `CC` , `CXX` , `LLVM_COV` , and `LLVM_PROFDATA` environment variables to Clang/LLVM compatible with the LLVM version used in rustc, and run cargo-llvm-cov with `--include-ffi` flag.\n```\nCC= < clang-path > \\\nCXX= < clang++-path > \\\nLLVM_COV= < llvm-cov-path > \\\nLLVM_PROFDATA= < llvm-profdata-path > \\\n  cargo llvm-cov --lcov --include-ffi\n```\n\nKnown compatible Rust (installed via rustup) and LLVM versions:\n\n| |Rust 1.60-1.77 |Rust 1.78-1.81 |Rust 1.82-1.92 |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n|LLVM 14-17 |**✓** | | |\n|LLVM 18 | |**✓** | |\n|LLVM 19-21 | | |**✓** |\n\n### Get coverage of external tests\n\n[]()\n\n`cargo test` , `cargo run` , and [`cargo nextest`](https://nexte.st/book/test-coverage.html) are available as builtin, but cargo-llvm-cov can also be used for arbitrary binaries built using cargo (including other cargo subcommands or external tests that use make, [xtask](https://github.com/matklad/cargo-xtask) , etc.) ```\n#  Set the environment variables needed to get coverage. source <( cargo llvm-cov show-env --export-prefix ) \n #  Remove artifacts that may affect the coverage results. #  This command should be called after show-env.\n ... \n```\n#  Set environment variables and clean workspace \n source <( cargo llvm-cov show-env --export-prefix ) \ncargo llvm-cov clean --workspace\n #  Build the fuzz target \ncargo afl build\n #  Run the fuzzer, the AFL_FUZZER_LOOPCOUNT is needed, because otherwise .profraw files aren't emitted \n #  To get coverage of current corpus, minimize it and set it as input, then run the fuzzer until it processes the corpus \nAFL_FUZZER_LOOPCOUNT=20 cargo afl fuzz -c - -i in -o out target/debug/fuzz-target\n #  Generate report \n #  If you pass `--release` to `cargo afl build`, you also need to pass `--release` to `cargo llvm-cov report` \ncargo llvm-cov report --lcov\n```\n\n### Exclude file from coverage\n\n[]()\n\nTo exclude specific file patterns from the report, use the `--ignore-filename-regex` option. ```\ncargo llvm-cov --open --ignore-filename-regex build\n```\n\nBy default, [vendored sources](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/commands/cargo-vendor.html) will not be included in the report.\nAlso, the following patterns and crates specified by `--exclude` or `--exclude-from-report` are excluded:\n\n```\n{SEPARATOR}rustc{SEPARATOR}([0-9a-f]+|[0-9]+\\.[0-9]+\\. [0-9]+){SEPARATOR}\n^{WORKSPACE_ROOT}({SEPARATOR}.*)? {SEPARATOR}(tests|examples|benches){SEPARATOR}\n^{TARGET_DIR}($|{SEPARATOR})\n^{CARGO_HOME}{SEPARATOR}(registry|git){SEPARATOR}\n^{RUSTUP_HOME}{SEPARATOR}toolchains($|{SEPARATOR})\n```\n\nYou can use `--disable-default-ignore-filename-regex` to disable default exclude setting. ### Exclude code from coverage\n\n[]()\n\nTo exclude the specific function or module from coverage, use the [`#[coverage(off)]` attribute](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/84605) . Since `#[coverage(off)]` is unstable, it is recommended to use it together with `cfg(coverage)` or `cfg(coverage_nightly)` set by cargo-llvm-cov. ```\n#!\n[ cfg_attr ( coverage_nightly ,  feature ( coverage_attribute ) ) ] \n\n // function \n # [ cfg_attr ( coverage_nightly ,  coverage ( off ) ) ] \n fn exclude_fn_from_coverage ( ) { \n    // ... \n } \n\n // module \n # [ cfg_attr ( coverage_nightly ,  coverage ( off ) ) ] \n mod exclude_mod_from_coverage { \n    // ... \n }\n```\n\ncfgs are set under the following conditions:\n\n* `cfg(coverage)` is always set when using cargo-llvm-cov (unless `--no-cfg-coverage` flag passed)\n* `cfg(coverage_nightly)` is set when using cargo-llvm-cov with nightly toolchain (unless `--no-cfg-coverage-nightly` flag passed)\n\nRust 1.80+ warns the above cfgs as `unexpected_cfgs` . The recommended way to address this is to add a [`lints` table](https://doc.rust-lang.org/nightly/rustc/check-cfg/cargo-specifics.html) to `Cargo.toml` . ```\n[ lints .\nrust ]\n unexpected_cfgs = { level = \" warn \" , check-cfg = [ ' cfg(coverage,coverage_nightly) ' ] }\n```\n\nIf you want to ignore all `#[test]` \\-related code, you can use module-level `#[coverage(off)]` attribute:\n\n```\n#! [ cfg_attr ( coverage_nightly ,  feature ( coverage_attribute ) ) ] \n\n # [ cfg ( test ) ] \n # [ cfg_attr ( coverage_nightly ,  coverage ( off ) ) ] \n mod tests { \n    // ... \n }\n```\n\ncargo-llvm-cov excludes code contained in the directory named `tests` from the report by default, so you can also use it instead of `#[coverage(off)]` attribute. ### Continuous Integration\n\n[]()\n\n#### GitHub Actions and Codecov\n\n[]()\n\nHere is an example of GitHub Actions workflow that uploads coverage to [Codecov](https://codecov.io) .\n```\nname : Coverage \n\n on : [pull_request, push] \n\n jobs :\n  coverage :\n    runs-on : ubuntu-latest \n    env :\n      CARGO_TERM_COLOR : always \n    steps :\n      - uses : actions/checkout@v5 \n      - name : Install Rust \n        run : rustup update stable \n      - name : Install cargo-llvm-cov \n        uses : taiki-e/install-action@cargo-llvm-cov \n      - name : Generate code coverage \n        run : cargo llvm-cov --all-features --workspace --lcov --output-path lcov.info \n      - name : Upload coverage to Codecov \n        uses : codecov/codecov-action@v5 \n        with :\n          token : ${{ secrets.CODECOV_TOKEN }} #  required for private repos or protected branches \n          files : lcov.info \n          fail_ci_if_error : true\n```\n\nCurrently, when using `--lcov` flag, [only line coverage is available on Codecov](https://github.com/taiki-e/cargo-llvm-cov/issues/20) .\n ... \ncargo-binstall\n\n[]()\n\nYou can install cargo-llvm-cov using [cargo-binstall](https://github.com/cargo-bins/cargo-binstall) :\n\n```\ncargo binstall cargo-llvm-cov\n```\n\n### Via pacman (Arch Linux)\n\n[]()\n\nYou can install cargo-llvm-cov from the [extra repository](https://archlinux.org/packages/extra/x86_64/cargo-llvm-cov) :\n\n```\npacman -S cargo-llvm-cov\n```\n\n### Via ports (FreeBSD)\n\n[]()\n\nYou can install cargo-llvm-cov from the [official ports](https://www.freshports.org/devel/cargo-llvm-cov) :\n\n```\npkg install cargo-llvm-cov\n```\n\n### Via other package managers\n\n[]()\n\n[](https://repology.org/project/cargo-llvm-cov/versions)\n\n## Known limitations\n\n[]()\n\n* Support for branch coverage is unstable. See [\\](https://github.com/taiki-e/cargo-llvm-cov/issues/8) and [rust-lang/rust](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/79649) for more. * Support for doc tests is unstable and has known issues.\nSee [\\](https://github.com/taiki-e/cargo-llvm-cov/issues/2) and [rust-lang/rust](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/79417) for more. See also [the code-coverage-related issues reported in rust-lang/rust](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/labels/A-code-coverage) . ## Related Projects\n\n[]()\n\n* [cargo-config2](https://github.com/taiki-e/cargo-config2) : Library to load and resolve Cargo configuration. cargo-llvm-cov uses this library. * [cargo-hack](https://github.com/taiki-e/cargo-hack) : Cargo subcommand to provide various options useful for testing and continuous integration. * [cargo-minimal-versions](https://github.com/taiki-e/cargo-minimal-versions) : Cargo subcommand for proper use of `-Z minimal-versions` . ## License\n\n[]()\n\nLicensed under either of [Apache License, Version 2.0](/taiki-e/cargo-llvm-cov/blob/main/LICENSE-APACHE) or [MIT license](/taiki-e/cargo-llvm-cov/blob/main/LICENSE-MIT) at your option.\nUnless you explicitly state otherwise, any contribution intentionally submitted\nfor inclusion in the work by you, as defined in the Apache-2.0 license, shall\nbe dual licensed as above, without any additional terms or conditions. ## About\n\nCargo subcommand to easily use LLVM source-based code coverage (-C instrument-coverage). ### Topics\n\n[rust](/topics/rust \"Topic: rust\") [cli](/topics/cli \"Topic: cli\") [coverage](/topics/coverage \"Topic: coverage\") [cargo](/topics/cargo \"Topic: cargo\")\n\n### Resources\n\n[Readme]()\n\n### License\n\n### Code of conduct\n\n[Code of conduct]()\n\n### Security policy\n\n[Security policy]()\n\n### Uh oh! There was an error while loading. Please reload this page ."

    "excerpts": [
      "[Skip to content]()\n\nYou signed in with another tab or window. Reload to refresh your session. You signed out in another tab or window. Reload to refresh your session. You switched accounts on another tab or window. Reload to refresh your session. Dismiss alert\n\n# Tracking issue for function attribute `#[coverage]` \\\n\n[New issue](/login?return_to=)\n\nCopy link\n\n[New issue](/login?return_to=)\n\nCopy link\n\nOpen\n\n0 / 1 0 of 1 issue completed\n\n[\\](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/pull/130766)\n\nOpen\n\n[Tracking issue for function attribute `#[coverage]`]() \\\n\n0 / 1 0 of 1 issue completed\n\n[\\](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/pull/130766)\n\nCopy link\n\nLabels\n\n[A-code-coverage Area: Source-based code coverage (-Cinstrument-coverage)](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues?q=state%3Aopen%20label%3A%22A-code-coverage%22) Area: Source-based code coverage (-Cinstrument-coverage) [B-unstable Blocker: Implemented in the nightly compiler and unstable.](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues?q=state%3Aopen%20label%3A%22B-unstable%22) Blocker: Implemented in the nightly compiler and unstable. [C-tracking-issue Category: An issue tracking the progress of sth. like the implementation of an RFC](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues?q=state%3Aopen%20label%3A%22C-tracking-issue%22) Category: An issue tracking the progress of sth. like the implementation of an RFC [T-lang Relevant to the language team](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues?q=state%3Aopen%20label%3A%22T-lang%22) Relevant to the language team\n\n[](https://github.com/richkadel)\n\n## Description\n\n[](https://github.com/richkadel)\n\n[richkadel](https://github.com/richkadel)\n\nopened [on Apr 27, 2021](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/84605)\n\nIssue body actions\n\nWarning\n\nThis main `#[coverage(..)]` attribute tracking issue description is quite outdated, and likely needs to be revisited.  \nPlease refer to [\\](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134749) for the stabilization issue for the `#[coverage(..)]` attribute.\n\nThis issue will track the approval and stabilization of the attribute `coverage` , needed to give developers a way to \"hide\" a function from the coverage instrumentation enabled by `rustc -Z instrument-coverage` .\n\nThe `Eq` trait in the `std` library implements a marker function that is not meant to be executed, but results in an uncovered region in all rust programs that derive `Eq` . This attribute will allow the compiler to skip that function, and remove the uncovered regions.\n\n### Unresolved questions\n\n* is it ok that the attribute applied to the crate root, a function, or a module applies to _all_ items within the marked item? So far we do not have such attributes other than the lint level attributes.\n\n### Path to stabilization\n\nPlease refer to the stabilization issue: [\\](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/134749) .\n\n👍 React with 👍 30 samuelcolvin, jsenkpiel-godaddy, JonathanWoollett-Light, meysam81, Defelo and 25 more\n\n### Sub-issues\n\n## Metadata\n\n## Metadata\n\n### Assignees\n\nNo one assigned\n\n### Labels\n\n[A-code-coverage Area: Source-based code coverage (-Cinstrument-coverage)](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues?q=state%3Aopen%20label%3A%22A-code-coverage%22) Area: Source-based code coverage (-Cinstrument-coverage) [B-unstable Blocker: Implemented in the nightly compiler and unstable.](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues?q=state%3Aopen%20label%3A%22B-unstable%22) Blocker: Implemented in the nightly compiler and unstable. [C-tracking-issue Category: An issue tracking the progress of sth. like the implementation of an RFC](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues?q=state%3Aopen%20label%3A%22C-tracking-issue%22) Category: An issue tracking the progress of sth. like the implementation of an RFC [T-lang Relevant to the language team](https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues?q=state%3Aopen%20label%3A%22T-lang%22) Relevant to the language team\n\n### Type\n\nNo type\n\n### Projects\n\nNo projects\n\n### Milestone\n\nNo milestone\n\n### Relationships\n\nNone yet\n\n### Development\n\nNo branches or pull requests\n\n## Issue actions\n\n## Footer\n\n[](https://github.com) © 2025 GitHub, Inc.\n\n### Footer navigation\n\n* [Terms](https://docs.github.com/site-policy/github-terms/github-terms-of-service)\n* [Privacy](https://docs.github.com/site-policy/privacy-policies/github-privacy-statement)\n* [Security](https://github.com/security)\n* [Status](https://www.githubstatus.com/)\n* [Docs](https://docs.github.com/)\n* [Contact](https://support.github.com?tags=dotcom-footer)\n* Manage cookies\n* Do not share my personal information\n\nYou can’t perform that action at this time.\n"

    "url": "https://llvm.org/docs/CommandGuide/llvm-cov.html",
    "title": "llvm-cov - emit coverage information",
    "excerpts": [
      "[](../index.html)\n\n### Navigation\n\n* [index](../genindex.html \"General Index\")\n* [next](llvm-cxxmap.html \"llvm-cxxmap - Mangled name remapping tool\") |\n* [previous](llvm-config.html \"llvm-config - Print LLVM compilation options\") |\n* [LLVM Home](https://llvm.org/) |\n* [Documentation](../index.html) »\n* [Reference](../Reference.html) »\n* [LLVM Command Guide](index.html) »\n* llvm-cov - emit coverage information\n\n### Documentation\n\n* [Getting Started/Tutorials](https://llvm.org/docs/GettingStartedTutorials.html)\n* [User Guides](https://llvm.org/docs/UserGuides.html)\n* [Reference](https://llvm.org/docs/Reference.html)\n\n### Getting Involved\n\n* [Contributing to LLVM](https://llvm.org/docs/Contributing.html)\n* [Submitting Bug Reports](https://llvm.org/docs/HowToSubmitABug.html)\n* [Mailing Lists](https://llvm.org/docs/GettingInvolved.html)\n* [Discord](https://llvm.org/docs/GettingInvolved.html)\n* [Meetups and Social Events](https://llvm.org/docs/GettingInvolved.html)\n\n### Additional Links\n\n*\n[FAQ](https://llvm.org/docs/FAQ.html)\n* [Glossary](https://llvm.org/docs/Lexicon.html)\n* [Publications](https://llvm.org/pubs)\n* [Github Repository](https://github.com/llvm/llvm-project/)\n\n### This Page\n\n* [Show Source](../_sources/CommandGuide/llvm-cov.rst.txt)\n\n### Quick search\n\n# llvm-cov - emit coverage information [¶]( \"Link to this heading\")\n\n## SYNOPSIS [¶]( \"Link to this heading\")\n\n**llvm-cov** _command_ [ _args…_ ]\n\n## DESCRIPTION [¶]( \"Link to this heading\")\n\nThe **llvm-cov** tool shows code coverage information for\nprograms that are instrumented to emit profile data. It can be used to\nwork with `gcov` \\-style coverage or with `clang` 's instrumentation\nbased profiling. If the program is invoked with a base name of `gcov` , it will behave as if\nthe **llvm-cov gcov** command were called. Otherwise, a command should\nbe provided.\n## COMMANDS [¶]( \"Link to this heading\")\n\n* [gcov]()\n* [show]()\n* [report]()\n* [export]()\n\n## GCOV COMMAND [¶]( \"Link to this heading\")\n\n### SYNOPSIS [¶]( \"Link to this heading\")\n\n**llvm-cov gcov** [ _options_ ] _SOURCEFILE_\n\n### DESCRIPTION [¶]( \"Link to this heading\")\n\nThe **llvm-cov gcov** tool reads code coverage data files and displays\nthe coverage information for a specified source file. It is compatible with the `gcov` tool from version 4.2 of `GCC` and may also be compatible with some\nlater versions of `gcov` . To use **llvm-cov gcov** , you must first build an instrumented version\nof your application that collects coverage data as it runs. Compile with the `-fprofile-arcs` and `-ftest-coverage` options to add the\ninstrumentation. (Alternatively, you can use the `--coverage` option, which\nincludes both of those other options.) At the time you compile the instrumented code, a `.gcno` data file will be\ngenerated for each object file.\n ... \nIf\na line contains code but that code was never executed, the count is displayed\nas `#####` . ### OPTIONS [¶]( \"Link to this heading\")\n\n\\-a , \\--all-blocks [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Display all basic blocks. If there are multiple blocks for a single line of\nsource code, this option causes llvm-cov to show the count for each block\ninstead of just one count for the entire line. \\-b , \\--branch-probabilities [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Display conditional branch probabilities and a summary of branch information. \\-c , \\--branch-counts [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Display branch counts instead of probabilities (requires -b). \\-m , \\--demangled-names [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Demangle function names. \\-f , \\--function-summaries [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Show a summary of coverage for each function instead of just one summary for\nan entire source file. \\--help [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Display available options (–help-hidden for more).\n ... \n\\-show-directory-coverage [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Generate an index file in each directory that contains at least one source\nfile with a top level index showing aggregates. Defaults to false. \\-use-color [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Enable or disable color output. By default this is autodetected. \\-arch =[\\*NAMES\\*] [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Specify a list of architectures such that the Nth entry in the list\ncorresponds to the Nth specified binary. If the covered object is a universal\nbinary, this specifies the architecture to use. It is an error to specify an\narchitecture that is not included in the universal binary or to use an\narchitecture that does not match a non-universal binary. \\-name =<NAME> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Show code coverage only for functions with the given name. \\-name-allowlist =<FILE> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Show code coverage only for functions listed in the given file.\nEach line in\nthe file should start with allowlist\\_fun: , immediately followed by the name\nof the function to accept. This name can be a wildcard expression. \\-name-regex =<PATTERN> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Show code coverage only for functions that match the given regular expression. \\-ignore-filename-regex =<PATTERN> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Skip source code files with file paths that match the given regular expression. \\-format =<FORMAT> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Use the specified output format. The supported formats are: “text”, “html”. \\-tab-size =<TABSIZE> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Replace tabs with <TABSIZE> spaces when preparing reports. Currently, this is\nonly supported for the html format. \\-output-dir =PATH [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Specify a directory to write coverage reports into. If the directory does not\nexist, it is created.\nWhen used in function view mode (i.e when -name or\n\\-name-regex are used to select specific functions), the report is written to\nPATH/functions.EXTENSION. When used in file view mode, a report for each file\nis written to PATH/REL\\_PATH\\_TO\\_FILE.EXTENSION. \\-Xdemangler =<TOOL>|<TOOL-OPTION> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Specify a symbol demangler. This can be used to make reports more\nhuman-readable. This option can be specified multiple times to supply\narguments to the demangler (e.g \\-Xdemangler c++filt -Xdemangler -n for C++). The demangler is expected to read a newline-separated list of symbols from\nstdin and write a newline-separated list of the same length to stdout. \\-num-threads =N , \\-j =N [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Use N threads to write file reports (only applicable when -output-dir is\nspecified). When N=0, llvm-cov auto-detects an appropriate number of threads to\nuse. This is the default.\n\\-compilation-dir =<dir> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Directory used as a base for relative coverage mapping paths. Only applicable\nwhen binaries have been compiled with one of \\-fcoverage-prefix-map \\-fcoverage-compilation-dir , or \\-ffile-compilation-dir . \\-line-coverage-gt =<N> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Show code coverage only for functions with line coverage greater than the\ngiven threshold. \\-line-coverage-lt =<N> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Show code coverage only for functions with line coverage less than the given\nthreshold. \\-region-coverage-gt =<N> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Show code coverage only for functions with region coverage greater than the\ngiven threshold. \\-region-coverage-lt =<N> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Show code coverage only for functions with region coverage less than the given\nthreshold.\n ... \n## REPORT COMMAND [¶]( \"Link to this heading\")\n\n### SYNOPSIS [¶]( \"Link to this heading\")\n\n**llvm-cov report** [ _options_ ] -instr-profile _PROFILE_ [ _BIN_ ] [ _\\-object BIN_ ]… [ _\\-sources_ ] [ _SOURCE_ ]…\n\n### DESCRIPTION [¶]( \"Link to this heading\")\n\nThe **llvm-cov report** command displays a summary of the coverage of\nthe binaries _BIN_ … using the profile data _PROFILE_ . It can optionally be\nfiltered to only show the coverage for the files listed in _SOURCE_ …. _BIN_ may be an executable, object file, dynamic library, or archive (thin or\notherwise). If no source files are provided, a summary line is printed for each file in the\ncoverage data. If any files are provided, summaries can be shown for each\nfunction in the listed files if the `-show-functions` option is enabled. For information on compiling programs for coverage and generating profile data,\nsee [SHOW COMMAND]() .\n### OPTIONS [¶]( \"Link to this heading\")\n\n\\-use-color [=VALUE] [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Enable or disable color output. By default this is autodetected. \\-arch =<name> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    If the covered binary is a universal binary, select the architecture to use. It is an error to specify an architecture that is not included in the\nuniversal binary or to use an architecture that does not match a\nnon-universal binary. \\-show-region-summary [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Show statistics for all regions. Defaults to true. \\-show-branch-summary [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Show statistics for all branch conditions. Defaults to true. \\-show-mcdc-summary [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Show MC/DC statistics. Defaults to false. \\-show-functions [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Show coverage summaries for each function. Defaults to false.\n\\-show-instantiation-summary [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Show statistics for all function instantiations. Defaults to false. \\-ignore-filename-regex =<PATTERN> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Skip source code files with file paths that match the given regular expression. \\-compilation-dir =<dir> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Directory used as a base for relative coverage mapping paths. Only applicable\nwhen binaries have been compiled with one of \\-fcoverage-prefix-map \\-fcoverage-compilation-dir , or \\-ffile-compilation-dir . \\-debuginfod [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Attempt to look up coverage mapping from objects using debuginfod. This is\nattempted by default for binary IDs present in the profile but not provided on\nthe command line, so long as debuginfod is compiled in and configured via\nDEBUGINFOD\\_URLS. \\-debug-file-directory =<dir> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Provides a directory to search for objects corresponding to binary IDs in the\nprofile.\n ... \nThe result will contain the same information as produced\nby the **llvm-cov report** command, but presented in JSON or lcov\nformat rather than text. \\-ignore-filename-regex =<PATTERN> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Skip source code files with file paths that match the given regular expression. \\-skip-expansions [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Skip exporting macro expansion coverage data. \\-skip-functions [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Skip exporting per-function coverage data. \\-num-threads =N , \\-j =N [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Use N threads to export coverage data. When N=0, llvm-cov auto-detects an\nappropriate number of threads to use. This is the default. \\-compilation-dir =<dir> [¶]( \"Link to this definition\")\n    Directory used as a base for relative coverage mapping paths. Only applicable\nwhen binaries have been compiled with one of \\-fcoverage-prefix-map \\-fcoverage-compilation-dir , or \\-ffile-compilation-dir ."
    ]
  },

[Learn more about Teams](https://stackoverflow.co/teams/)\n\n# [How can I get Rust code coverage to ignore unreachable lines? ](/questions/75186157/how-can-i-get-rust-code-coverage-to-ignore-unreachable-lines)\n\n[Ask Question](/questions/ask)\n\nAsked\n\nModified [2 years, 10 months ago](?lastactivity \"2023-01-20 21:01:31Z\")\n\nViewed 3k times\n\n1\n\n[](/posts/75186157/timeline \"Show activity on this post.\")\nUsing the following as an example:\n\n```\nfn get_num(input: i32) -> i32 {\n    input*2\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_it() {\n        for i in 1i32..72 {\n            match get_num(i) {\n                x if x%2 == 0 => { println! (\"even\"); }\n                _ => {println! (\"odd\");}\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\nand implementing the code coverage like so:\n\n```\nRUSTFLAGS=\"-C instrument-coverage\" cargo test;\nllvm-profdata merge -sparse default_*.profraw -o default.profdata;\nexecutable=$(RUSTFLAGS=\"-C instrument-coverage\" cargo test --no-run --message-format=json | \\\n  jq -r \"select(.profile.test == true) | .filenames[]\" | \\\n  \\grep -v dSY);\nllvm-cov show --instr-profile=default.profdata --object \"$executable\";\n```\n\nI would like the `println! (\"odd\");` not to be marked as \"not covered\". In javascript, I would use `istanbul-ignore-next` to ignore a line (mark them as \"exercised\"). In C#, one can use `ExcludeFromCodeCoverage` .\nUsing, `lcov` (for C or C++), you can use `LCOV_EXCL_LINE` . In python, use `# pragma: no cover` . In ruby, use `# :nocov:` . Is there a way to do this in Rust? * [rust](/questions/tagged/rust \"show questions tagged 'rust'\")\n* [code-coverage](/questions/tagged/code-coverage \"show questions tagged 'code-coverage'\")\n\n[Share](/q/75186157 \"Short permalink to this question\")\n\n[Improve this question](/posts/75186157/edit)\n\nFollow\n\n[edited Jan 20, 2023 at 19:51](/posts/75186157/revisions \"show all edits to this post\")\n\nasked Jan 20, 2023 at 15:32\n\n[](/users/200985/lmat-reinstate-monica)\n\n[lmat - Reinstate Monica](/users/200985/lmat-reinstate-monica)\n\n7,875 6 6 gold badges 54 54 silver badges 71 71 bronze badges\n\n1\n\n* 1\n  \n  In Rust, especially in production code, you should prefer not making it possible to encode states impossible to reach over having to handle impossible states with `unreachable! ()` code or alike.\njthulhu\n  \n  – [jthulhu](/users/5956261/jthulhu \"8,884 reputation\")\n  \n  2023-01-20 19:58:55 +00:00\n  \n  Commented Jan 20, 2023 at 19:58\n\n[Add a comment](# \"Use comments to ask for more information or suggest improvements. Avoid answering questions in comments.\") | [](# \"Expand to show all comments on this post\")\n\n## 3 Answers 3\n\nSorted by: [Reset to default](/questions/75186157/how-can-i-get-rust-code-coverage-to-ignore-unreachable-lines?answertab=scoredesc)\n\nHighest score (default) Trending (recent votes count more) Date modified (newest first) Date created (oldest first)\n\n1\n\n[](/posts/75186717/timeline \"Show activity on this post.\") Don't write unreachable code. If your test panics, it failed. If it doesn't panic, it succeeded. You don't need to examine output from the test to see if it succeeded or not. As such, don't use `match` ; use `assert_eq!` . ```\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_it() {\n        for i in 1i32..72 {\n            assert_eq!\n ... \n`#[no_coverage]` can be applied to functions, but is not yet stable: <https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/84605> . From that same ticket:\n\n> Ignoring a statement or block is not currently supported. > \n>\n\n[Share](/a/75189317 \"Short permalink to this answer\")\n\n[Improve this answer](/posts/75189317/edit)\n\nFollow\n\nanswered Jan 20, 2023 at 21:01\n\n[](/users/200985/lmat-reinstate-monica)\n\n[lmat - Reinstate Monica](/users/200985/lmat-reinstate-monica)\n\n7,875 6 6 gold badges 54 54 silver badges 71 71 bronze badges\n\n## Comments\n\nAdd a comment\n\n\\-2\n\n[](/posts/75186374/timeline \"Show activity on this post.\") That's not _generally_ possible because it would require for the compiler to do some super smart reasoning. In your particular case it's \"obvious\" that the match arm for odd numbers can't ever be reached, but what you're asking of the compiler (and the coverage tool) is to be a general _theorem prover_ .\n ... \nI would just like to be able to, for instance, `/* istanbul-ignore-next */`\n\n1 Reply\n\n* Copy link\n\n[](/users/381179/cadolphs)\n\ncadolphs\n\n[cadolphs](/users/381179/cadolphs) []()\n\nApologies, that wasn't quite clear from your question; it did sound indeed like you expected the compiler to realize that 2 \\* some\\_int is always going to be even...\n\n1 Reply\n\n* Copy link\n\n## Your Answer\n\n[](#)\n\nThanks for contributing an answer to Stack Overflow! * Please be sure to _answer the question_ . Regarding the coverage exclusion for data-only code: the `#[coverage(off)]` attribute requires nightly Rust and is unstable. For stable Rust, the practical options are:

1. **Use `--ignore-filename-regex`** in the coverage command to exclude specific files
2. **Accept the false positives** - the data-only files don't affect meaningful coverage metrics

**Tool Result:** `toolu_01TkV8hnMQ3bcXab8HyTXU8Q`

```
1) TL;DR
Create a small `config_paths` module that owns all filesystem paths under the editor’s config directory, then have `theme.rs`, `keymap/defaults.rs`, and `config.rs` call into it. Reuse the existing (correct) XDG logic from `theme.rs`, add helpers for common files, and move the “ensure directories exist” logic there.

---

2) Recommended approach (simple path)  **[Effort: M (1–3h)]**

### A. Add a new module for config paths

Create `src/config_paths.rs` (or similar) with a focused, self-contained API.

```rust
// src/config_paths.rs
use std::{
    env,
    fs,
    path::{Path, PathBuf},
};

const APP_DIR: &str = "token-editor";

/// Base config directory for token-editor
///
/// Unix/macOS:
///   - If XDG_CONFIG_HOME is set: $XDG_CONFIG_HOME/token-editor
///   - Else: ~/.config/token-editor
///
/// Windows:
///   - %APPDATA%\token-editor
pub fn config_dir() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    #[cfg(target_os = "windows")]
    {
        env::var("APPDATA")
            .ok()
            .map(|appdata| PathBuf::from(appdata).join(APP_DIR))
    }

    #[cfg(not(target_os = "windows"))]
    {
        // XDG-style: respect XDG_CONFIG_HOME, fall back to ~/.config
        env::var_os("XDG_CONFIG_HOME")
            .map(PathBuf::from)
            .or_else(|| dirs::home_dir().map(|h| h.join(".config")))
            .map(|config| config.join(APP_DIR))
    }
}

/// ~/.config/token-editor/themes/ (or %APPDATA%\token-editor\themes\)
pub fn themes_dir() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    config_dir().map(|dir| dir.join("themes"))
}

/// ~/.config/token-editor/config.yaml
pub fn config_file() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    config_dir().map(|dir| dir.join("config.yaml"))
}

/// ~/.config/token-editor/keymap.yaml
pub fn keymap_file() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    config_dir().map(|dir| dir.join("keymap.yaml"))
}

/// Ensure a directory exists
fn ensure_dir(path: &Path) -> Result<(), String> {
    fs::create_dir_all(path)
        .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to create directory {}: {}", path.display(), e))
}

/// Ensure the base config dir exists, returning it
pub fn ensure_config_dir() -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
    let dir = config_dir().ok_or_else(|| "No config directory available".to_string())?;
    ensure_dir(&dir)?;
    Ok(dir)
}

/// Ensure themes dir exists, returning it
pub fn ensure_themes_dir() -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
    let config = ensure_config_dir()?;
    let themes = config.join("themes");
    ensure_dir(&themes)?;
    Ok(themes)
}

/// Convenience: ensure full config structure (config dir + themes)
pub fn ensure_all_config_dirs() {
    match ensure_themes_dir() {
        Ok(themes) => {
            tracing::info!(
                "Config directories ready (themes dir: {})",
                themes.display()
            );
        }
        Err(e) => {
            tracing::warn!("Failed to ensure config directories: {}", e);
        }
    }
}
```

Expose it in `lib.rs` / `mod.rs`:

```rust
pub mod config_paths;
```

---

### B. Refactor `theme.rs` to use the centralized paths

At the top of `theme.rs`:

```rust
use crate::config_paths::{config_dir, themes_dir};
```

Replace the local path helpers:

1. Remove the existing `get_config_dir()` and `get_user_themes_dir()` implementations (lines 68–96).
2. Reintroduce thin wrappers (optional, for local naming consistency) that delegate to the central module:

```rust
/// Get the user's config directory for token-editor
pub fn get_config_dir() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    config_dir()
}

/// Get the user's theme configuration directory
///
/// Returns `~/.config/token-editor/themes/` on Unix
/// Returns `%APPDATA%\token-editor\themes\` on Windows
pub fn get_user_themes_dir() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    themes_dir()
}
```

3. All existing callers in `theme.rs` (e.g. `load_theme`, `list_available_themes`) will work as-is.

This keeps `theme.rs` behavior unchanged while centralizing the actual path logic.

---

### C. Refactor `keymap/defaults.rs` to use the centralized paths

At the top of `defaults.rs`:

```rust
use crate::config_paths::keymap_file;
```

Replace `get_user_config_path()` implementation (lines 21–39) with:

```rust
/// User keymap config path: ~/.config/token-editor/keymap.yaml
/// or %APPDATA%\token-editor\keymap.yaml
pub fn get_user_config_path() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    keymap_file()
}
```

The rest of the file can remain the same:

- `load_default_keymap()` already calls `get_user_config_path()`.
- The tests that assert properties of `get_user_config_path()` will still pass, but the implementation now uses the XDG-style logic via `config_paths::config_dir()`.

This also fixes the macOS behavior (no more `dirs::config_dir()` -> `~/Library/Application Support`).

---

### D. Refactor `config.rs` to use the centralized paths

At the top of `config.rs`, replace:

```rust
use crate::theme::get_config_dir;
```

with:

```rust
use crate::config_paths::{config_file, ensure_all_config_dirs};
```

Then:

1. Change `EditorConfig::config_path()` to delegate to the central module:

```rust
pub fn config_path() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    config_file()
}
```

2. Replace `EditorConfig::ensure_config_dirs()` with a simple wrapper to the central helper (or delete it and call the new helper from the application entrypoint):

Option A – keep the method but delegate:

```rust
pub fn ensure_config_dirs() {
    ensure_all_config_dirs();
}
```

Option B – if you prefer to avoid duplication, remove `EditorConfig::ensure_config_dirs()` and update the call sites to use `config_paths::ensure_all_config_dirs()` directly.

Behaviorally, this preserves what you had:

- Creates `~/.config/token-editor/` if missing.
- Creates `~/.config/token-editor/themes/` if missing.
- Logs via `tracing`.

3. `EditorConfig::save()` still uses `Self::config_path()` and now automatically benefits from the centralized logic.

---

3) Rationale and trade-offs

- **Single source of truth**: All OS- and env-specific logic for config locations lives in `config_paths.rs`. Any future config files (e.g., `snippets.yaml`) can re-use the same helpers.
- **Minimal refactor**: Existing public functions (`get_config_dir`, `get_user_config_path`, `EditorConfig::config_path`) keep the same signatures and semantics; they just delegate into the central module.
- **Requirements met**:
  - Unix/macOS: `~/.config/token-editor/` (with `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` honored).
  - Windows: `%APPDATA%\token-editor\`.
  - No `dirs::config_dir()` on macOS—only `dirs::home_dir()` for fallback.
  - Helpers for `config_dir`, `config.yaml`, `keymap.yaml`, `themes/`.
  - Explicit directory creation helpers.

The alternative would be to keep `get_config_dir()` in `theme.rs` as the canonical implementation and have others `use crate::theme::get_config_dir`, but that makes your config layout depend conceptually on the theme module. A dedicated `config_paths` module is cleaner and clearer.

---

4) Risks and guardrails

- **Non-standard macOS behavior**: Some macOS users may expect configs under `~/Library/Application Support`. You’re explicitly choosing XDG-style `~/.config`, which is fine but should be documented in user docs/README.
- **Missing home directory**: In odd environments (locked-down systems, restricted containers) `dirs::home_dir()` may return `None`. Currently you already handle this by returning `None`; keep that behavior and log a clear warning where needed.
- **Stringly-typed filenames**: Using string literals (`"config.yaml"`, `"keymap.yaml"`) in multiple places is still error-prone. The new module reduces this to a single place; ensure you don’t reintroduce ad-hoc joins elsewhere.

Guardrail: keep all future config-related path construction in `config_paths.rs`; if you see `".config"`/`"token-editor"` in other files, refactor back into the module.

---

5) When to consider the advanced path

Consider a more complex design if:

- You introduce **per-project** or **workspace-level** configs and need search/override resolution between project-level and user-level configs.
- You need **multi-app vendor directories** or support for different “profiles” (e.g., `token-editor-beta` alongside `token-editor`).
- You want **runtime overrides** (CLI flags, env vars) that change the base config directory.

At that point, a configuration “locator” type with a richer API (and maybe a builder) could be warranted.

---

6) Optional advanced path (if needed later)

An advanced design could introduce a `ConfigLocator` struct:

```rust
pub struct ConfigLocator {
    app_dir: String,
    base_dir: PathBuf,
}

impl ConfigLocator {
    pub fn new(app_dir: &str) -> Option<Self> { /* compute base_dir as above */ }
    pub fn config_file(&self) -> PathBuf { self.base_dir.join("config.yaml") }
    pub fn keymap_file(&self) -> PathBuf { self.base_dir.join("keymap.yaml") }
    pub fn themes_dir(&self) -> PathBuf { self.base_dir.join("themes") }
    pub fn ensure_all(&self) -> Result<(), String> { /* ... */ }
}
```

You’d construct it once at startup and pass references around, enabling things like different app IDs, dry-run modes, or temporary config roots. For now, this is unnecessary; the simple module-level helpers are enough and easier to integrate.
```

## Assistant

### Summary

Created a centralized [config_paths.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/config_paths.rs) module as the single source of truth for config paths:

**Key changes:**
1. **New `config_paths` module** with helpers:
   - `config_dir()` - `~/.config/token-editor/`
   - `keymap_file()` - `~/.config/token-editor/keymap.yaml`
   - `config_file()` - `~/.config/token-editor/config.yaml`
   - `themes_dir()` - `~/.config/token-editor/themes/`
   - `ensure_*` functions for directory creation

2. **Refactored all usages:**
   - `theme.rs` - delegates to `config_paths`
   - `keymap/defaults.rs` - fixed the bug (was using `dirs::config_dir()` → `~/Library/Application Support`)
   - `config.rs` - delegates to `config_paths`
   - `update/app.rs` - uses `config_paths`

3. **Fixed keymap file creation** - now copies the exact built-in keymap.yaml without adding any header

4. **Path logic:**
   - Unix/macOS: Respects `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, falls back to `~/.config/`
   - Windows: Uses `%APPDATA%`
   - Explicitly avoids `dirs::config_dir()` on macOS (which returns `~/Library/Application Support`)

### Summary

Removed all wrapper functions that just forwarded to `config_paths` and updated callers to use `config_paths` directly:

**Removed:**
- `theme::get_config_dir()`
- `theme::get_user_themes_dir()`
- `keymap::defaults::get_user_config_path()`

- `EditorConfig::ensure_config_dirs()`

**Updated callers to use `config_paths` directly:**
- [theme.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/theme.rs) - `load_theme()` and `list_available_themes()` now call `config_paths::themes_dir()`
- [keymap/defaults.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/keymap/defaults.rs) - `load_default_keymap()` now calls `config_paths::keymap_file()`
- [config.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/config.rs) - `load()` and `save()` now call `config_paths::config_file()`
- [model/mod.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/model/mod.rs) - startup calls `config_paths::ensure_all_config_dirs()`
- [update/app.rs](file://~/code/rust-editor/src/update/app.rs) - commands use `config_paths::config_dir()` and `config_paths::keymap_file()`

The `config_paths` module is now the single source of truth for all config paths.
